\displaystyle 2|\mathcal{E}|(cp)^{k}+\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\rho}\norm{\CQ\circ\tCQ\circ\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n'} \CW_j\right) \circ(\CE\otimes\CF) \circ\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n} \mathcal{N}_i\right)[\rho] - \CE[\rho]}_{1},

(171)

where first we have used the triangle inequality, then the data processing inequality, and lastly the fact that the
1
-norm is not greater than the diamond norm combined with the inequality in
Eq.

167
.
In what follows, we bound the second term by getting rid of the channels
\mathcal{N}_{i}
and invoking the QEC guarantee on the code
\mathcal{E}
to handle the channels
\mathcal{W}_{j}
.

From Ref.
[
59
, Theorem 59]
, we have that each
\mathcal{N}_{i}
acts on the
i
-th input qubit and

\mathcal{N}_{i}:\mathcal{B}(A_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(A_{i}\otimes F_{i})
, where
A_{i}
and
F_{i}
are Hilbert spaces associated with the
i
-th input qubit and the environment, respectively, and

\mathcal{B}(\cdot)
denotes linear operators on the Hilbert space. Note that in this notation,
\mathcal{E}:\mathcal{B}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}A_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}A_{i})
and
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{B}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}F_{i})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}F_{i})
.
Furthermore, it holds that

\mathrm{Tr}_{F_{i}}\circ\mathcal{N}_{i}=(1-\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{I}_{A_{i}}+\varepsilon_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{A_{i}},

(172)

where
\varepsilon_{0}\leq 2cp
for some absolute constant
c
,
\mathcal{Z}_{A_{i}}
is a CPTP map, and
\mathcal{I}_{X}
denotes the identity map on Hilbert space
X
.
Intuitively, this equation tells us that the channel
\mathcal{N}_{i}
is close to the identity channel when restricted to the
i
-th qubit, meaning that its action on the environment qubits must be close to a channel that appends a fixed reference state
\tau
.
This can be formalized with the continuity theorem of Stinespring’s dilation
[
130
, Theorem 1]
as follows.
First, consider the Stinespring dilation

\mathcal{N}_{i}[\cdot]=\Tr_{G_{i}}\ (V_{A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}[\cdot]V_{A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}^{\dagger})
, where
V_{A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}
is an isometry.
The continuity theorem states that for two quantum channels
\mathcal{T}_{1}
,
\mathcal{T}_{2}:\mathcal{B}(A)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(B)
, if
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are the Stinespring dilating isometries with the same dilating Hilbert space
E
, then

\displaystyle\inf_{U}\norm{(\Id_B \otimes U_E) V_1 - V_2}^{2}\leq\norm{\mathcal{T}_1 -\mathcal{T}_2}_{\diamond}\leq 2\inf_{U}\norm{(\Id_B \otimes U_E) V_1 - V_2},

(173)

where
\mathbb{I}_{X}
is the identity operator on Hilbert space
X
, and
\|\cdot\|
denotes the operator norm,
and the infimum is taken over unitaries
U
in
\mathcal{B}(E)
.
We apply this theorem by taking
A=B=A_{i}
,
E=F_{i}G_{i}
,

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\Tr_{F_{i}}\circ\ \mathcal{N}_{i}
,
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{I}_{A_{i}}
,
V_{1}=V_{A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}
and
V_{2}
to be an isometry that for all
\rho_{A_{i}}
satisfies
V_{2}\rho_{A_{i}}V_{2}^{\dagger}=\rho_{A_{i}}\otimes\tau_{F_{i}G_{i}}
for some fixed state
\tau_{F_{i}G_{i}}
.
Thus, the continuity theorem implies the existence of a unitary
U_{F_{i}G_{i}}
, such that

\displaystyle\norm{(\Id_{A_i} \otimes U_{F_iG_i}) V_{A_i\rightarrow A_iF_iG_i} - V_2}\leq\|\Tr_{F_{i}}\circ\ \mathcal{N}_{i}-\mathcal{I}_{A_{i}}\|_{\diamond}^{1/2}=\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{0}}.

(174)

It follows that for each
i
we can approximate
\mathcal{N}_{i}
with a channel
\mathcal{R}_{i}
defined by
\mathcal{R}_{i}[\rho_{A_{i}}]=\rho_{A_{i}}\otimes\sigma_{F_{i}}
, where
\sigma_{F_{i}}=\Tr_{G_{i}}(U_{F_{i}G_{i}}^{\dagger}\tau_{F_{i}G_{i}}U_{F_{i}G_{i}})
and satisfies

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}_{i}-\mathcal{R}_{i}\|_{\diamond}\leq 2\|V_{A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}F_{i}G_{i}}-(\mathbb{I}_{A_{i}}\otimes U^{\dagger}_{F_{i}G_{i}})V_{2}\|\leq 2\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{0}}\leq 4\sqrt{cp}.

(175)

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\norm{\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{N}_i - \bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\CR_i}_{\diamond}=\norm{\sum_{i=1}^{n} \CR_1 \otimes\dots\otimes\CR_{i-1} \otimes(\mathcal{N}_i-\CR_i) \otimes\mathcal{N}_{i+1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathcal{N}_n}_{\diamond}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\norm{\mathcal{N}_i-\CR_i}_{\diamond}\leq 4n\sqrt{cp},

(176)

allowing us to get rid of the channels
\mathcal{N}_{i}
in
Eq.

171
.
Namely, by invoking the triangle inequality and the fact that the 1-norm is not greater than the diamond norm we obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\rm enc}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\rho}\norm{\CQ\circ\tCQ\circ\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n'} \CW_j\right) \circ(\CE\otimes\CF) \circ\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\CR_i [\rho] - \CE[\rho]}_{1}+2n\sqrt{cp}+2|\mathcal{E}|(cp)^{k},

(177)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\rho}\norm{\CQ\circ\tCQ\circ\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n'} \CW_j\right) [\CE[\rho] \otimes\sigma] - \CE[\rho]}_{1}+2n\sqrt{cp}+2|\mathcal{E}|(cp)^{k}.

(178)

where we defined a state
\sigma=\mathcal{F}(\otimes_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{F_{i}})
on the Hilbert space
\otimes_{j=1}^{n^{\prime}}F_{j}
.

We now turn our attention to the channels
\mathcal{W}_{j}
.
From Ref.
[
59
, Theorem (59)]
, we know that each
\mathcal{W}_{j}
acts on the
j
-th output qubit and

\mathcal{W}_{j}:\mathcal{B}(A_{j}\otimes F_{j})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(A_{j})
, where
A_{j}
and
F_{j}
are Hilbert spaces associated with the
j
-th output qubit and the environment, respectively.
Furthermore, we have

\mathcal{W}_{j}=(1-\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{I}_{A_{j}}\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{F_{j}}+\varepsilon_{0}\mathcal{Y}_{A_{j}},

(179)

where
\varepsilon_{0}\leq 2cp
and
c
is the same absolute constant from
Eq.

172
and
\mathcal{Y}_{A_{j}}
is a CPTP map.
We would like to use the above form of
\mathcal{W}_{j}
and the stochastic-error decoding guarantee of the code
\mathcal{E}
to bound the first term in
Eq.

178
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n^{\prime}}\mathcal{W}_{j}\right)[\mathcal{E}[\rho]\otimes\sigma]

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\subseteq[n^{\prime}]}(1-\varepsilon_{0})^{n^{\prime}-|S|}\varepsilon_{0}^{|S|}\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}\circ\left(\bigotimes_{j\notin S}(\mathcal{I}_{j}\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{F_{j}})\bigotimes_{j^{\prime}\in S}\mathcal{Y}_{A_{j^{\prime}}}\right)[\mathcal{E}[\rho]\otimes\sigma]

(180)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\subseteq[n^{\prime}]}(1-\varepsilon_{0})^{n^{\prime}-|S|}\varepsilon_{0}^{|S|}\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}\circ(\mathcal{I}_{S^{c}}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{S})[\mathcal{E}[\rho]],

(181)

where
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{S}[\cdot]:=\left(\bigotimes_{j^{\prime}\in S}\mathcal{Y}_{A_{j^{\prime}}}\right)\Big[[\cdot]\otimes(\bigotimes_{j\notin S}\mathrm{Tr}_{F_{j}})[\sigma]\Big]
is a channel acting on the qubits
S\subseteq[n^{\prime}]
.
We now split the above sum into two parts depending whether or not the subset
S
is correctable. For a correctable set
S
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}\circ(\mathcal{I}_{S^{c}}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{S})[\mathcal{E}[\rho]]=\mathcal{E}[\rho].

(182)

On the other hand, according to the decoding guarantee of the fault-tolerant QEC gadget
\mathcal{Q}_{\rm FT}
on the code
\mathcal{E}
, as long as
p
(and accordingly
\varepsilon_{0}\leq 2cp
) is sufficiently below a threshold value, the contribution of the uncorrectable sets is bounded follows

\displaystyle p_{\text{uncorrectable}}:=\sum_{S\text{ uncorrectable}}(1-\varepsilon_{0})^{n^{\prime}-|S|}\varepsilon_{0}^{|S|}\leq\Gamma(\mathcal{E}),

(183)

where
\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
is a quantity that can be exponentially driven to zero by choosing larger codes from the QEC family, as discussed in
Section

3.3
(see Eq. (
18
)).
Hence, we have that

\displaystyle\norm{\CQ\circ\tCQ\circ\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{n'} \CW_j\right)[\CE[\rho] \otimes\sigma] - \CE[\rho]}_{1}

(184)

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle\norm{\sum_{ S \text{ uncorrectable}} (1-\varepsilon_0)^{n'-|S|} \varepsilon_0^{|S|} \CQ\circ\tCQ\circ(\mathcal{I}_{S^c} \otimes\CW'_S) [\CE[\rho]] - p_{\text{uncorrectable}}\cdot\CE[\rho]}_{1}

(185)

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle 2\Gamma(\mathcal{E}).

(186)

Collecting terms, we therefore conclude that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{enc}}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma(\mathcal{E})+2\sqrt{cp}n+2|\mathcal{E}|(cp)^{k},

(187)

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Delayed proofs for partial Clifford twirling

First, we state a couple of propositions used in the proofs of the claims in the main text.

Proposition 13
.

Suppose
P,P^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}
with
P\neq P^{\prime}
and
P\neq-P^{\prime}
. Let
\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}
denote expectation value over drawing
C
randomly from
\mathbb{T}
as in
Definition

4
. Then

\displaystyle\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}CPC^{\dagger}\otimes CP^{\prime}C^{\dagger}=0

(188)

Proof.

Denote
P
and
P^{\prime}
in canonical form as
P=\mathrm{i}^{a\cdot b}(-1)^{s}X^{b}Z^{a}
, and
P^{\prime}=\mathrm{i}^{a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}}(-1)^{s^{\prime}}X^{b^{\prime}}Z^{a^{\prime}}
. If
P\neq\pm P^{\prime}
, then it either must be the case that
a\neq a^{\prime}
or
b\neq b^{\prime}
. Consider a fixed
C\in\mathbb{T}
defined by
(A,B,u,v)
. We define another
C^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}
for each case separately. If
a\neq a^{\prime}
then
a\oplus a^{\prime}\neq 0^{n}
and we can find a
w
such that
w\cdot(a\oplus a^{\prime})=1
(note that
w
is actually independent of
C
). Then we define
C^{\prime}
by the choice
(A,B,u\oplus w,v)
. If
b\neq b^{\prime}
then we can find a
w
such that
w\cdot(A^{-1}b\oplus A^{-1}b^{\prime})=1
(
w
depends on
C
only through
A
), and we define
C^{\prime}
by
(A,B,u,v\oplus w)
. In both cases, referring to
Eq.

200
from the proof of
Proposition

4
, we have
CPC^{\dagger}=\pm C^{\prime}PC^{\prime{\dagger}}
and
CP^{\prime}C^{\dagger}=\mp C^{\prime}P^{\prime}C^{\prime{\dagger}}
, or in other words,

\displaystyle CPC^{\dagger}\otimes CP^{\prime}C^{\dagger}=-C^{\prime}PC^{\prime{\dagger}}\otimes C^{\prime}P^{\prime}C^{\prime{\dagger}}\,.

(189)

However, adding
w
to either
u
or to
v
does not change the random distribution over
C\sim\mathbb{T}
. This implies the expectation value is equal to its negation, and therefore it is zero.
∎

Proposition 14
.

Let
P\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm odd}
(from
Definition

5
). Then, for any
g
,

\displaystyle P|{\Psi(g)}\rangle\!\langle{\Psi(g)}|P|\Psi(g)\rangle=0\,.

(190)

Proof.

Write
P
in canonical form as
\mathrm{i}^{a\cdot b}(-1)^{s}X^{b}Z^{a}
. Since
P\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm odd}
, we have that
a\cdot b=1
. Hence, while
P
is Hermitian, it also satisfies
P=-P^{*}
, where the
∗
denotes complex conjugation. In other words, all of the matrix elements of
P
in the computational basis are imaginary. The Hermiticity of
P
implies that for any state
|\xi\rangle
, we have that
\langle\xi|P|\xi\rangle
is real, i.e.,
\langle\xi|P|\xi\rangle=(\langle\xi|P|\xi\rangle)^{*}
. However, if the entries of
|\xi\rangle
in the computational basis are real, then

\displaystyle(\langle\xi|P|\xi\rangle)^{*}=\langle\xi|P^{*}|\xi\rangle=-\langle\xi|P|\xi\rangle\,,

(191)

which implies that
\langle\xi|P|\xi\rangle=0
. It suffices to observe that the entries of
|\Psi(g)\rangle
are real.
∎

C.1
Proof of uniform Pauli spreading

Here we restate and prove
Proposition

4
from
Section

4.3
.
See
4

Proof.

Recall that choosing
C\sim\mathbb{T}
means generating uniformly random
A,B,u,v
and defining
C=Z^{v}Q_{B}M_{A}^{\dagger}X^{u}
. Let
P\in\mathbb{P}
be a fixed Pauli string
P=\mathrm{i}^{a\cdot b}(-1)^{s}X^{b}Z^{a}
. We examine the effect of conjugation by each component of
C
sequentially. We leave off the
\mathrm{i}
until the end since it is simply a constant.

1.

Conjugation by
X
gates: We have
X^{u}\big[(-1)^{s}X^{b}Z^{a}\big]X^{u}=(-1)^{s\oplus(u\cdot a)}X^{b}Z^{a}
.

2.

Conjugation by CNOT gates: First, we note that for any computational basis state
|x\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle M_{A}^{\dagger}X^{b}Z^{a}M_{A}|x\rangle

\displaystyle=M_{A}^{\dagger}X^{b}Z^{a}|Ax\rangle=(-1)^{Ax\cdot a}M_{A}^{\dagger}|Ax\oplus b\rangle=(-1)^{A^{\top}a\cdot x}|x\oplus A^{-1}b\rangle

(192)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{A^{\top}a\cdot x}X^{A^{-1}b}|x\rangle=X^{A^{-1}b}Z^{A^{\top}a}|x\rangle

(193)

From this, we have

\displaystyle M_{A}^{\dagger}\big[(-1)^{s\oplus(u\cdot a)}Z^{a}X^{b}\big]M_{A}=(-1)^{s\oplus(u\cdot a)}X^{A^{-1}b}Z^{A^{\top}a}

(194)

3.

Conjugation by
\mathrm{CZ}
: The
Q_{B}
gate is diagonal and Hermitian and commutes with Pauli-
Z
strings. We notice for any
p\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
and any
|x\rangle
that

\displaystyle Q_{B}X^{p}Q_{B}|x\rangle

\displaystyle=(-1)^{x^{\top}Bx}Q_{B}X^{p}|x\rangle=(-1)^{x^{\top}Bx}Q_{B}|x\oplus p\rangle=(-1)^{\big[x^{\top}Bx\big]\oplus\big[(x\oplus p)^{\top}B(x\oplus p)\big]}|x\oplus p\rangle

(195)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\big[x^{\top}Bx\big]\oplus\big[(x\oplus p)^{\top}B(x\oplus p)\big]}X^{p}|x\rangle=(-1)^{\big[(Bp\oplus B^{\top}p)\cdot x\big]\oplus\big[p^{\top}Bp\big]}X^{p}|x\rangle

(196)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{p^{\top}Bp}X^{p}Z^{(Bp\oplus B^{\top}p)}|x\rangle

(197)

and thus

\displaystyle Q_{B}\Big[(-1)^{s\oplus(u\cdot a)}X^{A^{-1}b}Z^{A^{\top}a}\Big]Q_{B}=(-1)^{s\oplus\big[u\cdot a\big]\oplus\big[b^{\top}A^{-1\top}BA^{-1}b\big]}X^{A^{-1}b}Z^{A^{\top}a\oplus\big[(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b\big]}

(198)

4.

Conjugation by
Z
: Finally, the
Z^{v}
gate is commuted, which only modifies the sign

\displaystyle Z^{v}\Big[\mathrm{i}^{a\cdot b}(-1)^{s\oplus\big[u\cdot a\big]\oplus\big[b^{\top}A^{-1\top}BA^{-1}b\big]}X^{A^{-1}b}Z^{A^{\top}a\oplus\big[(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b\big]}\Big]Z^{v}

(199)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}^{a\cdot b}(-1)^{s\oplus\big[u\cdot a\big]\oplus\big[b^{\top}A^{-1\top}BA^{-1}b\big]\oplus\big[v\cdot A^{-1}b\big]}X^{A^{-1}b}Z^{A^{\top}a\oplus\big[(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b\big]}

(200)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle CPC^{\dagger}

(201)

Now, we verify that the claims of the uniformity of the distribution over
Q=CPC^{\dagger}
when
A,B,u,v
are chosen uniformly at random. We begin with the easy cases.

•

If
P\in\mathbb{P}_{0}
or
P\in\mathbb{P}_{1}
, then the statement is trivial, as the subset has only one element, and substituting
a=b=0^{n}
confirms that
CPC^{\dagger}=P
.

•

If
P\in\mathbb{P}_{Z}
, then
b=0^{n}
and
a\neq 0^{n}
. We have

\displaystyle CPC^{\dagger}=\mathrm{i}^{a\cdot b}(-1)^{s\oplus\big[u\cdot a\big]}Z^{A^{\top}a}

(202)

For
A
a uniformly random invertible binary matrix,
A^{\top}a
is a uniformly random nonzero element of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
. Moreover, the uniformly random choice of
u\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
ensures that the sign is uniformly random. Together, these facts confirm that
CPC^{\dagger}
is uniformly random over
\mathbb{P}_{Z}
.

To handle the final two cases, we now examine the term
(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b
. We have defined
B
as a random upper triangular matrix. Note, however, that for the matrix
B\oplus B^{\top}
, one obtains the same distribution regardless of whether
B
is upper triangular or arbitrary, so long as the entries of
B
are chosen uniformly at random from
\mathbb{F}_{2}
. Furthermore, if
V
is an invertible matrix, then
B\mapsto V^{\top}BV
is a bijective map (its inverse is
B\mapsto V^{\top-1}BV^{-1}
). Thus, if
B
is chosen uniformly at random from all
n\times n
matrices in
\mathbb{F}_{2}
then the distribution over
V^{\top}BV
is also the uniform distribution over all matrices in
\mathbb{F}_{2}
, so we may replace
B
with
V^{\top}BV
and not change the distribution.
For fixed
A
and nonzero
b
, we can find an invertible
V
such that
VA^{-1}b=e
, where
e=(1,0,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
is the string with a single 1 in its first entry. For this choice of
V
, we have

\displaystyle(V^{\top}BV\oplus V^{\top}B^{\top}V)A^{-1}b=V^{\top}(B\oplus B^{\top})e

(203)

We observe that
(B\oplus B^{\top})e
has a zero in its first entry, regardless of the choice of
B
, but the other
n-1
entires are uniformly random when
B
is uniformly random, that is,
(B\oplus B^{\top})e
is distributed uniformly over the subset of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
orthogonal to
e
(in the sense of having dot product zero). Letting
c=(B\oplus B^{\top})e
, we have
0=c^{\top}e=(V^{\top}c)^{\top}V^{-1}e=(V^{\top}c)^{\top}A^{-1}b
, so
A^{-1}b
is orthogonal to
V^{\top}c
. In fact, since
c
is distributed uniformly over all vectors orthogonal to
e
and
V
is bijective,
V^{\top}c
is distributed uniformly over all vectors orthogonal to
A^{-1}b
. As noted above, since
V
is a bijective map, the distribution of
V^{\top}BV
is the same as that of
B
itself, so we can replace
V^{\top}BV
with
B
in
Eq.

203
, and hence we see that
(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b=V^{\top}c
. Thus, the quantity
(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b
is also distributed uniformly over all vectors orthogonal to
A^{-1}b
.
We now continue with the final two cases.

•

If
P\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm odd}
, then
a,b\neq 0^{n}
(otherwise
a\cdot b=1
would be impossible). The quantity
CPC^{\dagger}
can be written in canonical form as
\mathrm{i}^{a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}}(-1)^{s^{\prime}}X^{b^{\prime}}Z^{a^{\prime}}
, where
s^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}
,
a^{\prime}
are given by
Eq.

200
. First, we immediately observe that randomizing over
u\in\mathbb{F}^{n}
leads
u\cdot a
to be uniformly random, and thus
s^{\prime}
is uniformly random over
\mathbb{F}_{2}
, independent of
a^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}
. Since
A
is distributed randomly over invertible matrices,
b^{\prime}=A^{-1}b
is distributed uniformly at random over
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}\setminus\{0^{n}\}
. Next, for fixed
A
(and thus fixed
b^{\prime}
), the above reasoning establishes that,

\displaystyle a^{\prime}=A^{\top}a\oplus\big[(B\oplus B^{\top})A^{-1}b\big]=A^{\top}a\oplus r

(204)

where
r
is distributed uniformly at random over vectors orthogonal to
b^{\prime}=A^{-1}b
. Recall that
(A^{\top}a)\cdot(A^{-1}b)=a\cdot b=1
. This means that

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}=\big[(A^{\top}a)\cdot(A^{-1}b)\big]\oplus\big[r\cdot A^{-1}b\big]=a\cdot b=1

(205)

Since
r
is distributed at random among all vectors for which
b^{\prime}\cdot r=0
, the distribution over
a^{\prime}
is precisely the uniform distribution over all vectors for which
a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}=1
. This confirms that
CPC^{\dagger}
is distributed uniformly over
\mathbb{P}_{\rm odd}
.

•

If
P\in\mathbb{P}_{\rm even}
, then
b\neq 0^{n}
and
a\cdot b=0
. As before, we have
CPC^{\dagger}=\mathrm{i}^{a^{\prime}\cdot b^{\prime}}(-1)^{s^{\prime}}X^{b^{\prime}}Z^{a^{\prime}}
, and
b^{\prime}=A^{-1}b
is distributed uniformly at random over
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}\setminus\{0^{n}\}
. Furthermore, since
v\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
is uniformly and indepenently chosen, the quantity
v\cdot(A^{-1}b)
is uniformly random, meaning
s^{\prime}
is uniformly random and independent of
a^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}
. Next, we can again write

\displaystyle a^{\prime}=A^{\top}a\oplus r

(206)

where
r
is orthogonal to
b^{\prime}
, and
A^{\top}a
is a fixed vector for which
b^{\prime}\cdot(A^{\top}a)=b\cdot a=0
. Thus, the distribution over
a^{\prime}
is uniform over vectors orthogonal to
b^{\prime}
, confirming that
CPC^{\dagger}
is uniform over the set
\mathbb{P}_{\rm even}
.

∎

C.2
Proof of correct top eigenvector

Here we restate and prove
Proposition

5
from
Section

4.3
.
See
5

Proof.

From the definition of dataset-independent noise (
Definition

1
) and the definiton of
\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}
in
Eq.

25
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}=\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\rm FT}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{E}}}_{\rm FT}\circ\mathcal{N}_{2}\circ\mathcal{V}(g)\circ\mathcal{N}_{1}[|{+}\rangle\!\langle{+}|^{\otimes n}]\,.

(207)

Since the ideal encdoing map
\mathcal{E}
is injective and
\mathcal{Q}
projects onto its image (i.e., the codespace), we may define the channel
\mathcal{N}_{\rm enc}=\mathcal{E}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\rm FT}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{E}}}_{\rm FT}
(where
\mathcal{E}^{-1}
is well defined on inputs in the codespace) and note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\rm FT}\circ\widetilde{\vphantom{\raisebox{1.4pt}{:}}\smash[t]{\mathcal{E}}}_{\rm FT}=\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}_{\rm enc}\,.

(208)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}=\mathcal{E}\Big[\mathcal{N}_{\rm enc}\Big[\mathcal{N}_{2}\Big[V(g)\,\mathcal{N}_{1}\Big[|{+}\rangle\!\langle{+}|^{\otimes n}\Big]V(g)\Big]\Big]\Big]

(209)

The
2^{n}
states
\{Z^{u}|+\rangle,u\in\{0,1\}^{n}\}
form an orthonormal basis, and we may write the state
\mathcal{N}_{1}\Big[|{+}\rangle\!\langle{+}|^{\otimes n}\Big]
as a fixed
g
-independent density matrix in this basis

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}\Big[|{+}\rangle\!\langle{+}|^{\otimes n}\Big]=\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{n}}M_{uv}Z^{u}|{+}\rangle\!\langle{+}|^{\otimes n}Z^{v}

(210)

The operation
V(g)
is diagonal and commutes with
Z^{u}
and
Z^{v}
, so we have

\displaystyle V(g)\Big[\mathcal{N}_{1}\Big[|{+}\rangle\!\langle{+}|^{\otimes n}\Big]\Big]V(g)=\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{n}}M_{uv}Z^{u}|{\Psi(g)}\rangle\!\langle{\Psi(g)}|Z^{v}

(211)

The channel
\mathcal{N}_{\rm enc}\circ\mathcal{N}_{2}
in general has a Pauli decomposition

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\rm enc}\circ\mathcal{N}_{2}[\sigma]=\sum_{P,P^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}}\chi_{PP^{\prime}}P\sigma P^{\prime}

(212)

where
\chi
is some noise matrix, and
\mathbb{P}
denotes the set of signed Pauli strings, as in
Definition

5
. Moreover,
\mathcal{E}
is the physical-to-logical mapping which is equivalent to putting an overline on all the objects.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}=\sum_{P,P^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}}\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\chi_{PP^{\prime}}M_{uv}\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{u}|{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}\rangle\!\langle{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}|\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{v}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997 (213)

Now we compute the expectation over the twirled state, recalling the equality from
Proposition

3
.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}_{\rm twirl}

\displaystyle=\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}\sum_{P,P^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}}\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\chi_{PP^{\prime}}M_{uv}\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{u}|{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}}\rangle\!\langle{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g_{C})$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g_{C})}}}}|\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{v}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}^{\prime}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}

(214)

\displaystyle=\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}\sum_{P,P^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}}\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\chi_{PP^{\prime}}M_{uv}\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{u}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}|{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}\rangle\!\langle{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}|\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{ (215)

\displaystyle=\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}\sum_{P,P^{\prime}\in\mathbb{P}}\chi^{\prime}_{PP^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}|{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}\rangle\!\langle{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}|\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}^{\prime}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}

(216)

where we have absorbed the
\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{u}
and
\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$Z$}}\smash[t]{Z}}}^{v}
into
\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}
and
\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P^{\prime}$}}\smash[t]{P^{\prime}}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P^{\prime}$}}\smash[t]{P^{\prime}}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P^{\prime}$}}\smash[t]{P^{\prime}}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P^{\prime}$}}\smash[t]{P^{\prime}}}}
, leading to a redefinition of
\chi_{PP^{\prime}}
to
\chi^{\prime}_{PP^{\prime}}
. We may move the expectation value inside the sum. The result of
Proposition

13
ensures that if
P\neq P^{\prime}
, the expectation value vanishes, leaving only diagonal terms where
P=P^{\prime}
(if
P=-P^{\prime}
then the term does not immediately vanish, but without loss of generality we may take
\chi^{\prime}_{-P,P}=0
for all
P
by appropriately redefining the value of
\chi^{\prime}_{P,P}
). This gives

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}_{\rm twirl}=\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}}\chi^{\prime}_{PP}\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}|{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}\rangle\!\langle{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}|\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}\,.

(217)

Recall from
Definition

5
that we can partition the set
\mathbb{P}
into sets
\mathbb{P}_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}_{1}
,
\mathbb{P}_{Z}
,
\mathbb{P}_{\rm even}
, and
\mathbb{P}_{\rm odd}
. Note that
\mathbb{P}_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}_{1}
each contain a single element, which differs by a sign that cancels out in the expression above. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\chi^{\prime}_{PP}=0
for
P=-\mathbb{I}
and ignore the set
\mathbb{P}_{1}
henceforth. The fact that
\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\phi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\phi(g)}}}_{\rm twirl}
has trace 1 means that
\sum_{P\in\mathbb{P}}\chi^{\prime}_{PP}=1
; we define
\chi^{\prime}_{0}
,
\chi^{\prime}_{Z}
,
\chi^{\prime}_{\rm even}
, and
\chi^{\prime}_{\rm odd}
as the sum of
\chi^{\prime}_{PP}
over
P
from the relevant set.

The result of
Proposition

4
states that for each
P
,
CPC^{\dagger}
is distributed uniformly over the subset of
\mathbb{P}
that contains
P
. Thus, for all
P
in the same subset the quantity
\EV_{C\sim\mathbb{T}}\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}^{\dagger}|{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}\rangle\!\langle{\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$\Psi(g)$}}\smash[t]{\Psi(g)}}}}|\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$C$}}\smash[t]{C}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}{\overline{\vphantom{\raisebox{-0.39993pt}{$P$}}\smash[t]{P}}}\,\mathchoice{\overline{\vphanto
